<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the effective Iitaka fibration conjecture in characteristic zero. For each dimension, one pluricanonical degree defines the Iitaka fibration of every smooth integral projective variety of that dimension and nonnegative Kodaira dimension over an algebraically closed field. The associated sections generate the full Iitaka function field.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For each integer $d\geq1$ there is an integer $m(d)>0$ such that, over every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, the complete system $\|m(d)K_X\|$ defines the Iitaka fibration of every smooth integral projective $d$-fold $X$ with $\kappa(X)\geq0$.
  - Explanation: One pluricanonical degree depending only on dimension generates the full Iitaka field for every variety in the stated regime.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-lc-index)): Let $d\geq0$, and let $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$ satisfy the descending chain condition. There is an integer $a(d,\Phi)>0$ such that every projective log canonical pair $(X,B)$ of dimension $d$, with boundary coefficients in $\Phi$ and $K_X+B\sim_\mathbb Q0$, satisfies $$a(d,\Phi)(K_X+B)\sim0.$$ Here the multiple is an integral principal divisor, not merely a numerically trivial or rationally linearly trivial divisor.
  - Explanation: A common multiple depending on dimension and the rational DCC coefficient set makes every stated lc adjoint an integral principal divisor.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-good-models)): Let $(X,B)$ be a projective lc pair over $\mathbb C$ with effective rational boundary and pseudo-effective $K_X+B$. Then $(X,B)$ has a good log minimal model. In particular, its adjoint is nonvanishing, and on the good model the adjoint is semiample.
  - Explanation: The companion abundance input supplies a good log minimal model, nonvanishing and semiampleness.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-vertical-descent)): Let $f:X\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective varieties over a field of characteristic zero. A rational function on $X$ whose divisor has no horizontal component belongs to the subfield $k(Z)\subset k(X)$. Suppose, in addition, that $(X,B)$ is a rational pair with $K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, and that $q(K_{X_\eta}+B_\eta)\sim0$ on the generic fibre. There are a rational $q$-canonical form $s$ and a rational Cartier divisor $D\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ on $Z$ such that $$\label{eq:index-vertical-form} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}(s)+qB=f^*D.$$
  - Explanation: Vertical principal divisors descend to the base field, and a fibre trivialization produces an exact base pullback.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-lc-reduction)): Assume $L_j$ for every $j<n$ and $K_n$. Then $L_n$ holds: for every finite set $\Gamma\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$ there is an integer $c(n,\Gamma)>0$ such that $$c(n,\Gamma)(K_X+B)\sim0$$ for every normal projective lc $n$-fold pair $(X,B)$ with coefficients in $\Gamma$ and $K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$.
  - Explanation: Lower-dimensional normal lc bounds and the zero-boundary klt bound imply the normal lc bound in the next dimension.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-character)): Assume $L_j$ in a fixed dimension $j$. There is an integer $c_j>0$ with the following property. Let $(V,E)$ be a normal projective lc $j$-fold pair, with $E$ reduced, and let $\omega$ be a rational top form satisfying $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\omega)+E=0$. If $\gamma$ is a finite-order automorphism of the pair and $\gamma^*\omega=\lambda\omega$, then $\lambda^{c_j}=1$.
  - Explanation: The lower-dimensional lc index hypothesis bounds finite-order log volume characters.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-index-character)): Let $\pi:R\to X$ be a finite Galois quasi-étale cover of normal projective varieties, with $K_R\sim0$. Choose a generating reflexive top form $\omega$ on $R$, and write $g^*\omega=\chi(g)\omega$ for the Galois action. Then $$mK_X\sim0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\chi^m=1.$$
  - Explanation: The canonical principal multiples downstairs are exactly the powers killing the Galois volume character.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cover-comparison)): Let $k$ be a field of characteristic zero and let $P=\prod_{i=1}^t B_i$ be a product of geometrically integral projective factors whose geometric fibres are positive dimensional blocks as above; include the abelian part, when present, as a single factor. Let $R\to P$ be a geometrically integral normal finite quasi-étale cover, and put $d=\dim R$. If a finite group $G$ acts on $R$, then there is a subgroup $G_0$ of index at most $d!$ and connected finite quasi-étale covers $R_i\to B_i$ with the following properties:* 1. *$R\to\prod_iR_i$ is finite and quasi-étale;* 2. *$G_0$ acts regularly on each $R_i$, and the projections $R\to R_i$ are equivariant;* 3. *a generating volume form on $R$ is proportional to the wedge product of the pulled-back volume generators on the $R_i$.* *The conclusions also hold for finite semilinear actions, interpreting a factor transformation as an isomorphism to the corresponding field-conjugate variety. In particular the $d!$th power of any finite-order transformation preserves all factor fields.
  - Explanation: A subgroup of bounded index preserves the factor fields and acts regularly on their Stein covers, including semilinear actions.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-denominator)): There are positive integers $p_0\mid p$, depending only on $n$, with the following property. Let $f:X\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties, where $\dim X=n$, $\dim Z>0$, $X$ is klt, and $K_X$ is rationally linearly equivalent to the pullback of a rational Cartier divisor on $Z$. Then there are a rational function $\psi\in\mathbb C(X)^*$ and canonical bundle formula data satisfying the equality of rational divisors $$\label{eq:den-exact} K_X+\frac{1}{p_0}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(\psi)=f^*D_Z, \qquad D_Z=K_Z+B_Z+M_Z.$$ Here $B_Z\geq0$ has coefficients in a rational DCC set depending only on $n$, the generalized pair $(Z,B_Z+M_Z)$ is generalized klt, and the moduli b-divisor $\mathbf M$ is b-nef with $p\mathbf M$ b-Cartier.
  - Explanation: The zero-boundary klt fibration has an exact canonical bundle formula with a uniformly bounded moduli denominator.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-den-weight-rules)): The weight in <a href="#eq:den-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:den-weight">[eq:den-weight]</a> has the following properties.* 1. *Finite extension of the fibre function field, with the base field fixed, does not change the weight of a pulled-back form.* 2. *Under a curve extension of ramification $l$ at the marked point, with new parameter $u$, the pulled-back weight is multiplied by $l$.* 3. *Taking a positive tensor power leaves the weight unchanged. Multiplying a degree-$m$ form by a base function $a$ adds $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_z(a)/m$ to its weight.
  - Explanation: Relative form weights transform predictably under field extensions, ramification, tensor powers and base functions.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-den-characters)): In the semistable construction above, let $R_i$ be the Stein block covers, of dimensions less than $n$, over the final curve field. Let $c$ be the marked point, $u$ its uniformizer, and $g$ a finite-order lift of base inertia. Put $b=n!$, so that $g^b$ preserves each block, and choose volume generators $\eta_i$ with $w_u(\eta_i)=0$. There is an integer $N=N(n)>0$ such that, writing $$(g^b)^*\eta_i=c_i\eta_i, \qquad \lambda_i=c_i(c),$$ each $c_i$ is a unit at $c$ and $\lambda_i^N=1$.
  - Explanation: Normalized volume generators on degenerating Stein blocks have uniformly bounded central characters.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-big-base)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:denominator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:denominator">10</a>, suppose that $D_Z$ is big. There is a positive integer $m=m(n)$, divisible by $p_0$, such that $\|mK_X\|$ is nonempty and its section-ratio field inside $\mathbb C(X)$ is exactly $\mathbb C(Z)$.
  - Explanation: Bigness of the base adjoint gives a uniform pluricanonical degree recovering its full function field.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-rc-torsion)): Fix a dimension $d$, a rational DCC set $I\subset[0,1]$, and a positive integer $p$. There is an integer $\ell=\ell(d,I,p)>0$ with the following property. Let $Z$ be a normal projective complex variety of dimension $d$ with a rationally connected smooth projective resolution. Suppose $(Z,B+M_Z)$ is generalized klt, $B\geq0$ has coefficients in $I$, the b-nef data satisfy $p\mathbf M$ b-Cartier, and $$D=K_Z+B+M_Z\sim_{\mathbb Q}0.$$ Then $\ell D$ is an integral principal divisor.
  - Explanation: A rationally trivial generalized klt adjoint with fixed nef denominator on a rationally connected base has a common principal multiple.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-relative-rc-torsion)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:denominator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:denominator">10</a>, suppose $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ and a smooth projective resolution of $Z$ is rationally connected. There is an integer $r=r(n)>0$ such that $rK_X$ is an integral principal divisor.
  - Explanation: Rational linear triviality of the total canonical divisor and rational connectedness of the resolved base give a uniform canonical principal multiple.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-index-reduction)): If the canonical indices of projective klt $n$-folds with $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ are unbounded, there is a sequence of projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal $n$-folds $V_i$, of canonical indices $r_i\to\infty$, satisfying the following properties.* 1. *A smooth projective resolution of $V_i$ has irregularity zero, and $\mathop{\mathrm{Bir}}(V_i)$ is countable.* 2. *Every positive-dimensional rational image of $V_i$ of dimension less than $n$ has rationally connected smooth projective resolution.* 3. *Every effective Weil divisor on $V_i$ with positive Iitaka dimension is big.* 4. *Let $\pi_i:Y_i\to V_i$ be the cyclic index cover, with its deck group $G_i$. Then $Y_i$ is terminal and $K_{Y_i}\sim0$. Every $G_i$-equivariant rational fibration of $Y_i$ having positive-dimensional base and fibre has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. The same assertion holds for rational fibrations of $V_i$, with trivial group action.* *Here a rational fibration has geometrically integral generic fibre, or equivalently has a relatively algebraically closed base function field.
  - Explanation: Unbounded klt canonical indices would force terminal counterexamples with countable birational groups and restrictive intermediate fibrations.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-single-block-images)): Suppose that a projective klt $n$-fold $X$ with $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ has a finite quasi-étale cover by one irreducible Calabi–Yau or irreducible holomorphic symplectic block $P$. Then a smooth resolution of $X$ has irregularity zero, and every positive-dimensional rational image of dimension less than $n$ has rationally connected smooth projective resolution.
  - Explanation: A single nonabelian block cover forces vanishing irregularity and rational connectedness of lower-dimensional rational images.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-general-type-fibres)): Let $V$ be projective, $\mathbb Q$-factorial and terminal, with $K_V\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, and suppose that every effective Weil divisor of positive Iitaka dimension is big. Let $\pi:Y\to V$ be its cyclic index cover with deck group $G$. Every intermediate $G$-equivariant rational fibration of $Y$ has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. The same is true on $V$ without a group action.
  - Explanation: Bigness of every moving effective Weil divisor forces general-type geometric generic fibres of intermediate fibrations.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar)): Assume $I_j$ for $j<n$. There are constants $C_n,c_n>0$ with the following property. Let $V$ be a normal projective canonical $n$-fold with $K_V\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, and let a finite group $G$ act on $V$. Suppose that every $G$-equivariant rational fibration with geometrically integral generic fibre and positive-dimensional base and fibre has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. If $P$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor with $g^*P\sim_{\mathbb Q}P$ for every $g\in G$, then $$\label{eq:scalar-estimates} \gamma(P;V)\le C_n(P^n)^{1/n}, \qquad \varepsilon(P)\ge c_n(P^n)^{1/n}.$$ The constants are independent of $V$, $G$, and the denominator of $P$.
  - Explanation: The lower-dimensional Iitaka hypothesis gives uniform upper section-order and lower Seshadri estimates under the general-type fibration condition.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chain-input)): For a finite nonempty list of marked covering families on an integral projective variety in characteristic zero, there is a rational fibration $\xi:V\dashrightarrow M$ with geometrically integral generic fibre. If its generic leaf has dimension $h$, then $1\le h\le\dim V$, and every generic member is contained in its leaf. From a geometric generic mark $a$, a path of at most $h$ generic movements has endpoint generic in that leaf over $\overline{k_*(a)}$. The construction is equivariant for any finite group preserving the list. A dominant rational map carrying upper movements to lower movements or stationary steps carries upper leaves into lower leaves. For a finite group quotient $V\to V'$, the images of the leaves of an invariant list are the generic leaves of a rational fibration on $V'$.* *Let $P$ be big, nef and semiample. Suppose each geometric generic marked member satisfies either of the following bounds, with the same number $B$:* 1. *its mark $u(b)$ is smooth on $C_b$ and $\gamma(P\|_{C_b};C_b,u(b))\le B$;* 2. *it is a curve and $P\cdot C_b/\operatorname{mult}_{u(b)}C_b\le B$.* *Let $H$ be a smooth projective resolution of the geometric generic leaf, and retain pullback notation for $P$. Then $$\label{eq:chain-input-bounds} \gamma(P;H)\le hB, \qquad P^hH\le(hB)^h.$$
  - Explanation: Generic chains generate rational leaves, with order and degree controlled by the marked member bounds.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tracking-input)): Let $Z$ be projective, $\mathbb Q$-factorial and klt of dimension $d\ge2$, and let $N$ be big, nef and semiample. Suppose that $d+1$ positive rational numbers with consecutive spacing $\Delta>0$ lie strictly between $(N^d)^{1/d}$ and $\gamma(N;Z)$. Then there is a covering family of integral subvarieties $S$ of dimension $0<e<d$ whose smooth geometric generic resolution $S^*$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} N^eS&\le(2/\Delta)^{d-e}N^d,\label{eq:tracking-degree}\\ K_{S^*}(N\|_{S^*})^{e-1} &\le\bigl(K_Z+(2d/\Delta)N\bigr)\|_S(N\|_S)^{e-1}. \label{eq:tracking-canonical} \end{aligned}$$ In addition, if a projective variety has a smooth model with nonnegative Kodaira dimension, then a smooth projective model of the geometric generic member of any covering family of integral subvarieties has nonnegative Kodaira dimension. The same statement holds with “general type” in place of “nonnegative Kodaira dimension.”
  - Explanation: Excess section orders produce covering subvarieties with controlled polarized canonical degree; canonical dimension persists in covering members.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-small-fibre-volume)): Fix $1\le e<n$ and $C'>0$. Let $(W_i,L_i)$ be a sequence of smooth projective $e$-folds with $\kappa(W_i)\ge0$ and big, nef, semiample rational Cartier divisors $L_i$, such that $$\label{eq:small-volume-hypotheses} L_i^e\longrightarrow0, \qquad K_{W_i}L_i^{e-1}\le C'L_i^e.$$ Assume $I_e$. After a subsequence and resolution of the Iitaka fibrations, their smooth geometric generic fibres $U_i$ have fixed positive dimension, satisfy $\kappa(U_i)=0$, and obey $$\label{eq:small-fibre-volume} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(K_{U_i}+L_i\|_{U_i})\longrightarrow0.$$ The restricted polarizations are big, nef and semiample. If the Iitaka base is a point, $U_i$ denotes the entire smooth model.
  - Explanation: Vanishing polarized volume with controlled canonical degree produces positive-dimensional Iitaka fibres with vanishing adjoint volume.
- **C23** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-diagonal)): Let $V$ be a normal projective canonical complex variety of dimension $n\ge2$, with $K_V\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ and canonical index $r$. Let $\pi:Y\to V$ be its canonical cyclic cover. Suppose that $L$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor and $C_1>0$ is rational, with $$1\le L^n\le2,\qquad \gamma(L;V)\le C_1.$$ Choose a positive rational number $b_0$ such that $$\label{eq:diag-b0} 3(2b_0)^n<\frac14,\qquad b_0(C_1+1)<\frac34.$$ For every integer $t\ge2$, set $$Z_t=Y^t/\mu_{r,\mathrm{diag}},\qquad \theta_t:Z_t\longrightarrow V^t,\qquad P_t=\theta_t^*L^{\boxplus t}.$$ Then $P_t$ is ample and $$\label{eq:diag-epsilon} \varepsilon(P_t)\le\frac{(n+1)t^2}{b_0}.$$
  - Explanation: Diagonal products of the cyclic index cover have a Seshadri upper bound quadratic in the number of factors.
- **C24** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-diag-slopes)): Let $C$ be a smooth projective curve of genus $g$ over an algebraically closed field of characteristic $p>0$. If $E$ has rank $\rho$ and $\mu_{\min}(E)\ge0$, then, with $$G=\max\{0,2g-2\},\qquad u=\rho(p-1),\qquad c_2=\rho(\rho-1)G,$$ every nonzero graded piece satisfies $$\label{eq:diag-truncated-slope} \mu_{\max}(A_j(E))\le(p-1)\deg E+c_2, \qquad 0\le j\le u.$$ The sum of their ranks is $p^\rho$.
  - Explanation: Truncated symmetric powers admit a maximum-slope bound and an exact total-rank count in positive characteristic.
- **C25** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-diag-flag)): Let $W$ be a smooth projective $n$-fold, let $H$ be an ample rational Cartier divisor, and fix a smooth complete intersection flag cut successively by divisors in $\|l_iH\|$, $1\le i<n$, ending in a smooth integral curve $C$. Suppose that the $l_iH$ are integral ample divisors. Set $d_i=l_iH\cdot C$. If a vector bundle $E$ of rank $e$ satisfies $\mu_{\max}(E\|_C)\le M$ with $M\ge0$, then $$\label{eq:diag-flag-bound} h^0(W,E)\le e(M+1)\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}(1+M/d_i).$$
  - Explanation: A complete intersection flag converts a curve slope bound into a global section bound.
- **C26** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-diag-orders)): Let $W_i$ be integral projective varieties, $x_i$ smooth points, and $Q_i$ line bundles with nonzero section spaces, for $1\le i\le t$. Set $a_i=\max_{0\ne s\in H^0(W_i,Q_i)}\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{x_i}s$. Every nonzero section of $\bigotimes_i\operatorname{pr}_i^*Q_i$ has order at most $\sum_i a_i$ at $(x_1,\ldots,x_t)$.
  - Explanation: The maximum section order of an external product is bounded by the sum of the slot maxima.
- **C27** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-index-contradiction)): There is no sequence of finite surjective covers $\pi_\nu:Y_\nu\to V_\nu$ of normal projective complex $n$-folds, generically torsors for cyclic groups $G_\nu$ of orders $r_\nu\to\infty$, satisfying the following conditions. The groups $\mathop{\mathrm{Bir}}(V_\nu)$ are countable, and there are ample rational Cartier divisors $L_\nu$ and constants $c,C_2>0$, independent of $\nu$, such that $$\varepsilon(L_\nu)\ge c,\qquad \varepsilon(\pi_\nu^*L_\nu)\ge cr_\nu^{1/n}.$$ For $t\ge2$, let $Z_{\nu,t}=Y_\nu^t/G_{\nu,\mathrm{diag}}$, let $\theta_{\nu,t}:Z_{\nu,t}\to V_\nu^t$ be the finite quotient map, and set $P_{\nu,t}=\theta_{\nu,t}^*L_\nu^{\boxplus t}$. The final condition is $$\varepsilon(P_{\nu,t})\le C_2t^2$$ for every fixed $t$, on a tail allowed to depend on $t$. All Seshadri constants are at very general points.
  - Explanation: Compatible low-degree leaves would produce uncountably many birational self-maps, contradicting the countability hypothesis.
- **C28** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-all-degree)): Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex variety with pseudo-effective $K_X$. There is a projective terminal good minimal model $V$ of $X$. For every integer $m\geq0$, its natural birational identification of function fields identifies $$H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(mK_X))=H^0(V,\mathcal O_V(mK_V)).$$ The right-hand side is a divisorial section space even when $mK_V$ is not Cartier.
  - Explanation: All pluricanonical degrees agree birationally with divisorial section spaces on a terminal good minimal model.

Let $(V,E)$ be a normal projective lc $j$-fold pair, with $E$ reduced, and let $\\omega$ be a rational top form satisfying $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\omega)+E=0$. If $\\gamma$ is a finite-order automorphism of the pair and $\\gamma^*\\omega=\\lambda\\omega$, then $\\lambda^{c_j}=1$.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-character"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-index-character",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:R\\to X$ be a finite Galois quasi-étale cover of normal projective varieties, with $K_R\\sim0$.         "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cover-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a field of characteristic zero and let $P=\\prod_{i=1}^t B_i$ be a product of geometrically integral projective factors whose geometric fibres are positive dimensional blocks as above; include the abelian part, when present, as a single factor. Let $R\\to P$ be a geometrically integral normal finite quasi-étale cover, and put $d=\\dim R$. If a finite group $G$ acts on $R$, then there is a subgroup $G_0$ of index at most $d!$ and connected finite quasi-étale covers $R_i\\to B_i$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$R\\to\\prod_iR_i$ is finite and quasi-étale;*\n\n2.  *$G_0$ acts regularly on each $R_i$, and the projections $R\\to R_i$ are equivariant;*\n\n3.  *a generating volume form on $R$ is proportional to the wedge product of the pulled-back volume generators on the $R_i$.*\n\n*The conclusions also hold for finite semilinear actions, interpreting a factor transformation as an isomorphism to the corresponding field-conjugate variety. In particular the $d!$th power of any finite-order transformation preserves all factor fields.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-denominator",
        "exact_quote": "There are positive integers $p_0\\mid p$, depending only on $n$, with the following property. Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties, where $\\dim X=n$, $\\dim Z>0$, $X$ is klt, and $K_X$ is rationally linearly equivalent to the pullback of a rational Cartier divisor on $Z$. Then there are a rational function $\\psi\\in\\mathbb C(X)^*$ and canonical bundle formula data satisfying the equality of rational divisors $$\\label{eq:den-exact}\n K_X+\\frac{1}{p_0}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\psi)=f^*D_Z,\n \\qquad D_Z=K_Z+B_Z+M_Z.$$ Here $B_Z\\geq0$ has coefficients in a rational DCC set depending only on $n$, the generalized pair $(Z,B_Z+M_Z)$ is generalized klt, and the moduli b-divisor $\\mathbf M$ is b-nef with $p\\mathbf M$ b-Cartier.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-denominator",
          "exact_quote": "There are positive integers $p_0\\mid p$, depending only on $n$, with the following property. Then there are a rational function $\\psi\\in\\mathbb C(X)^*$ and canonical bundle formula data satisfying the equality of rational divisors $$\\label{eq:den-exact}\n K_X+\\frac{1}{p_0}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\psi)=f^*D_Z,\n \\qquad D_Z=K_Z+B_Z+M_Z.$$ Here $B_Z\\geq0$ has coefficients in a rational DCC set depending only on $n$, the generalized pair $(Z,B_Z+M_Z)$ is generalized klt, and the moduli b-divisor $\\mathbf M$ is b-nef with $p\\mathbf M$ b-Cartier.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-den-weight-rules",
        "exact_quote": "The weight in <a href=\"#eq:den-weight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:den-weight\">[eq:den-weight]</a> has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Finite extension of the fibre function field, with the base field fixed, does not change the weight of a pulled-back form.*\n\n2.  *Under a curve extension of ramification $l$ at the marked point, with new parameter $u$, the pulled-back weight is multiplied by $l$.*\n\n3.  *Taking a positive tensor power leaves the weight unchanged. Multiplying a degree-$m$ form by a base function $a$ adds $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_z(a)/m$ to its weight.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-den-weight-rules",
          "exact_quote": "The weight in <a href=\"#eq:den-weight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:den-weight\">[eq:den-weight]</a> has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Finite extension of the fibre function field, with the base field fixed, does not change the weight of a pulled-back form.*\n\n2.  *Under a curve extension of ramification $l$ at the marked point, with new parameter $u$, the pulled-back weight is multiplied by $l$.*\n\n3.  *Taking a positive tensor power leaves the weight unchanged. Multiplying a degree-$m$ form by a base function $a$ adds $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_z(a)/m$ to its weight.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The weight in <a href=\"#eq:den-weight\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:den-weight\">[eq:den-weight]</a> has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Finite extension of the fibre function field, with the base field fixed, does not change the weight of a pulled-back form.*\n\n2.  *Under a curve extension of ramification $l$ at the marked point, with new parameter $u$, the pulled-back weight is multiplied by $l$.*\n\n3.  *Taking a positive tensor power leaves the weight unchanged. Multiplying a degree-$m$ form by a base function $a$ adds $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_z(a)/m$ to its weight.",
      "explanation": "Relative form weights transform predictably under field extensions, ramification, tensor powers and base functions.",

Multiplying a degree-$m$ form by a base function $a$ adds $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_z(a)/m$ to its weight.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-den-characters",
        "exact_quote": "In the semistable construction above, let $R_i$ be the Stein block covers, of dimensions less than $n$, over the final curve field. Let $c$ be the marked point, $u$ its uniformizer, and $g$ a finite-order lift of base inertia. Put $b=n!$, so that $g^b$ preserves each block, and choose volume generators $\\eta_i$ with $w_u(\\eta_i)=0$. There is an integer $N=N(n)>0$ such that, writing $$(g^b)^*\\eta_i=c_i\\eta_i,\n \\qquad \\lambda_i=c_i(c),$$ each $c_i$ is a unit at $c$ and $\\lambda_i^N=1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-den-characters",
          "exact_quote": "In the semistable construction above, let $R_i$ be the Stein block covers, of dimensions less than $n$, over the final curve field.           "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-rc-torsion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a dimension $d$, a rational DCC set $I\\subset[0,1]$, and a positive integer $p$. There is an integer $\\ell=\\ell(d,I,p)>0$ with the following property. Let $Z$ be a normal projective complex variety of dimension $d$ with a rationally connected smooth projective resolution. Suppose $(Z,B+M_Z)$ is generalized klt, $B\\geq0$ has coefficients in $I$, the b-nef data satisfy $p\\mathbf M$ b-Cartier, and $$D=K_Z+B+M_Z\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0.$$ Then $\\ell D$ is an integral principal divisor.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rc-torsion",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a dimension $d$, a rational DCC set $I\\subset[0,1]$, and a positive integer $p$. There is an integer $\\ell=\\ell(d,I,p)>0$ with the following property. Let $Z$ be a normal projective complex variety of dimension $d$ with a rationally connected smooth projective resolution. Suppose $(Z,B+M_Z)$ is generalized klt, $B\\geq0$ has coefficients in $I$, the b-nef data satisfy $p\\mathbf M$ b-Cartier, and $$D=K_Z+B+M_Z\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0.$$ Then $\\ell D$ is an integral principal divisor.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"

      "exact_statement": "Fix a dimension $d$, a rational DCC set $I\\subset[0,1]$, and a positive integer $p$. There is an integer $\\ell=\\ell(d,I,p)>0$ with the following property.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-index-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "If the canonical indices of projective klt $n$-folds with $K_X\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$ are unbounded, there is a sequence of projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal $n$-folds $V_i$, of canonical indices $r_i\\to\\infty$, satisfying the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A smooth projective resolution of $V_i$ has irregularity zero, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bir}}(V_i)$ is countable.*\n\n2.  *Every positive-dimensional rational image of $V_i$ of dimension less than $n$ has rationally connected smooth projective resolution.*\n\n3.  *Every effective Weil divisor on $V_i$ with positive Iitaka dimension is big.*\n\n4.  *Let $\\pi_i:Y_i\\to V_i$ be the cyclic index cover, with its deck group $G_i$. Then $Y_i$ is terminal and $K_{Y_i}\\sim0$. Every $G_i$-equivariant rational fibration of $Y_i$ having positive-dimensional base and fibre has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. The same assertion holds for rational fibrations of $V_i$, with trivial group action.*\n\n*Here a rational fibration has geometrically integral generic fibre, or equivalently has a relatively algebraically closed base function field.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-index-reduction",

Then $Y_i$ is terminal and $K_{Y_i}\\sim0$. Every $G_i$-equivariant rational fibration of $Y_i$ having positive-dimensional base and fibre has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. The same assertion holds for rational fibrations of $V_i$, with trivial group action.*\n\n*Here a rational fibration has geometrically integral generic fibre, or equivalently has a relatively algebraically closed base function field.",

        "exact_quote": "Suppose that a projective klt $n$-fold $X$ with $K_X\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$ has a finite quasi-étale cover by one irreducible Calabi–Yau or irreducible holomorphic symplectic block $P$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-general-type-fibres",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be projective, $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and terminal, with $K_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$, and suppose that every effective Weil divisor of positive Iitaka dimension is big. Let $\\pi:Y\\to V$ be its cyclic index cover with deck group $G$. Every intermediate $G$-equivariant rational fibration of $Y$ has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. The same is true on $V$ without a group action.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $I_j$ for $j<n$. There are constants $C_n,c_n>0$ with the following property. Let $V$ be a normal projective canonical $n$-fold with $K_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$, and let a finite group $G$ act on $V$. Suppose that every $G$-equivariant rational fibration with geometrically integral generic fibre and positive-dimensional base and fibre has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. If $P$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor with $g^*P\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}P$ for every $g\\in G$, then $$\\label{eq:scalar-estimates}\n       \\gamma(P;V)\\le C_n(P^n)^{1/n},\n       \\qquad \\varepsilon(P)\\ge c_n(P^n)^{1/n}.$$ The constants are independent of $V$, $G$, and the denominator of $P$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar",
          "exact_quote": "Assume $I_j$ for $j<n$. There are constants $C_n,c_n>0$ with the following property. Let $V$ be a normal projective canonical $n$-fold with $K_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$, and let a finite group $G$ act on $V$. Suppose that every $G$-equivariant rational fibration with geometrically integral generic fibre and positive-dimensional base and fibre has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. If $P$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor with $g^*P\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}P$ for every $g\\in G$, then $$\\label{eq:scalar-estimates}\n       \\gamma(P;V)\\le C_n(P^n)^{1/n},\n       \\qquad \\varepsilon(P)\\ge c_n(P^n)^{1/n}.$$ The constants are independent of $V$, $G$, and the denominator of $P$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Assume $I_j$ for $j<n$. There are constants $C_n,c_n>0$ with the following property.       "explanation": "The lower-dimensional Iitaka hypothesis gives uniform upper section-order and lower Seshadri estimates under the general-type fibration condition.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-chain-input",
        "exact_quote": "For a finite nonempty list of marked covering families on an integral projective variety in characteristic zero, there is a rational fibration $\\xi:V\\dashrightarrow M$ with geometrically integral generic fibre. If its generic leaf has dimension $h$, then $1\\le h\\le\\dim V$, and every generic member is contained in its leaf. From a geometric generic mark $a$, a path of at most $h$ generic movements has endpoint generic in that leaf over $\\overline{k_*(a)}$. The construction is equivariant for any finite group preserving the list. A dominant rational map carrying upper movements to lower movements or stationary steps carries upper leaves into lower leaves. For a finite group quotient $V\\to V'$, the images of the leaves of an invariant list are the generic leaves of a rational fibration on $V'$.*\n\n*Let $P$ be big, nef and semiample. Suppose each geometric generic marked member satisfies either of the following bounds, with the same number $B$:*\n\n1.  *its mark $u(b)$ is smooth on $C_b$ and $\\gamma(P|_{C_b};C_b,u(b))\\le B$;*\n\n2.  *it is a curve and $P\\cdot C_b/\\operatorname{mult}_{u(b)}C_b\\le B$.*\n\n*Let $H$ be a smooth projective resolution of the geometric generic leaf, and retain pullback notation for $P$. Then $$\\label{eq:chain-input-bounds}\n                         \\gamma(P;H)\\le hB,\n                         \\qquad P^hH\\le(hB)^h.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-chain-input",
          "exact_quote": "For a finite nonempty list of marked covering families on an integral projective variety in characteristic zero, there is a rational fibration $\\xi:V\\dashrightarrow M$ with geometrically integral generic fibre. For a finite group quotient $V\\to V'$, the images of the leaves of an invariant list are the generic leaves of a rational fibration on $V'$.*\n\n*Let $P$ be big, nef and semiample. Suppose each geometric generic marked member satisfies either of the following bounds, with the same number $B$:*\n\n1.  *its mark $u(b)$ is smooth on $C_b$ and $\\gamma(P|_{C_b};C_b,u(b))\\le B$;*\n\n2.  *it is a curve and $P\\cdot C_b/\\operatorname{mult}_{u(b)}C_b\\le B$.*\n\n*Let $H$ be a smooth projective resolution of the geometric generic leaf, and retain pullback notation for $P$. Then $$\\label{eq:chain-input-bounds}\n                         \\gamma(P;H)\\le hB,\n                         \\qquad P^hH\\le(hB)^h.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C20",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-chain-input",
          "exact_quote": "For a finite nonempty list of marked covering families on an integral projective variety in characteristic zero, there is a rational fibration $\\xi:V\\dashrightarrow M$ with geometrically integral generic fibre. If its generic leaf has dimension $h$, then $1\\le h\\le\\dim V$, and every generic member is contained in its leaf. From a geometric generic mark $a$, a path of at most $h$ generic movements has endpoint generic in that leaf over $\\overline{k_*(a)}$. The construction is equivariant for any finite group preserving the list. A dominant rational map carrying upper movements to lower movements or stationary steps carries upper leaves into lower leaves. For a finite group quotient $V\\to V'$, the images of the leaves of an invariant list are the generic leaves of a rational fibration on $V'$.*\n\n*Let $P$ be big, nef and semiample. Suppose each geometric generic marked member satisfies either of the following bounds, with the same number $B$:*\n\n1.  *its mark $u(b)$ is smooth on $C_b$ and $\\gamma(P|_{C_b};C_b,u(b))\\le B$;*\n\n2.  *it is a curve and $P\\cdot C_b/\\operatorname{mult}_{u(b)}C_b\\le B$.*\n\n*Let $H$ be a smooth projective resolution of the geometric generic leaf, and retain pullback notation for $P$. Then $$\\label{eq:chain-input-bounds}\n                         \\gamma(P;H)\\le hB,\n                         \\qquad P^hH\\le(hB)^h.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-chain-input"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tracking-input",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be projective, $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and klt of dimension $d\\ge2$, and let $N$ be big, nef and semiample. Suppose that $d+1$ positive rational numbers with consecutive spacing $\\Delta>0$ lie strictly between $(N^d)^{1/d}$ and $\\gamma(N;Z)$. Then there is a covering family of integral subvarieties $S$ of dimension $0<e<d$ whose smooth geometric generic resolution $S^*$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n N^eS&\\le(2/\\Delta)^{d-e}N^d,\\label{eq:tracking-degree}\\\\\n K_{S^*}(N|_{S^*})^{e-1}\n &\\le\\bigl(K_Z+(2d/\\Delta)N\\bigr)|_S(N|_S)^{e-1}.\n \\label{eq:tracking-canonical}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In addition, if a projective variety has a smooth model with nonnegative Kodaira dimension, then a smooth projective model of the geometric generic member of any covering family of integral subvarieties has nonnegative Kodaira dimension. The same statement holds with “general type” in place of “nonnegative Kodaira dimension.”",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-fibre-volume",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\le e<n$ and $C'>0$. Let $(W_i,L_i)$ be a sequence of smooth projective $e$-folds with $\\kappa(W_i)\\ge0$ and big, nef, semiample rational Cartier divisors $L_i$, such that $$\\label{eq:small-volume-hypotheses}\n                  L_i^e\\longrightarrow0,\n                  \\qquad K_{W_i}L_i^{e-1}\\le C'L_i^e.$$ Assume $I_e$. After a subsequence and resolution of the Iitaka fibrations, their smooth geometric generic fibres $U_i$ have fixed positive dimension, satisfy $\\kappa(U_i)=0$, and obey $$\\label{eq:small-fibre-volume}\n                     \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(K_{U_i}+L_i|_{U_i})\\longrightarrow0.$$ The restricted polarizations are big, nef and semiample. If the Iitaka base is a point, $U_i$ denotes the entire smooth model.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-diagonal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a normal projective canonical complex variety of dimension $n\\ge2$, with $K_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$ and canonical index $r$. Let $\\pi:Y\\to V$ be its canonical cyclic cover. Suppose that $L$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor and $C_1>0$ is rational, with $$1\\le L^n\\le2,\\qquad \\gamma(L;V)\\le C_1.$$ Choose a positive rational number $b_0$ such that $$\\label{eq:diag-b0}\n 3(2b_0)^n<\\frac14,\\qquad b_0(C_1+1)<\\frac34.$$ For every integer $t\\ge2$, set $$Z_t=Y^t/\\mu_{r,\\mathrm{diag}},\\qquad\n \\theta_t:Z_t\\longrightarrow V^t,\\qquad\n P_t=\\theta_t^*L^{\\boxplus t}.$$ Then $P_t$ is ample and $$\\label{eq:diag-epsilon}\n \\varepsilon(P_t)\\le\\frac{(n+1)t^2}{b_0}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-diagonal",

          "source_id": "PM-034.10"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diag-slopes",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective curve of genus $g$ over an algebraically closed field of characteristic $p>0$. If $E$ has rank $\\rho$ and $\\mu_{\\min}(E)\\ge0$, then, with $$G=\\max\\{0,2g-2\\},\\qquad\n u=\\rho(p-1),\\qquad c_2=\\rho(\\rho-1)G,$$ every nonzero graded piece satisfies $$\\label{eq:diag-truncated-slope}\n \\mu_{\\max}(A_j(E))\\le(p-1)\\deg E+c_2,\n \\qquad 0\\le j\\le u.$$ The sum of their ranks is $p^\\rho$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diag-flag",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth projective $n$-fold, let $H$ be an ample rational Cartier divisor, and fix a smooth complete intersection flag cut successively by divisors in $|l_iH|$, $1\\le i<n$, ending in a smooth integral curve $C$. Suppose that the $l_iH$ are integral ample divisors. Set $d_i=l_iH\\cdot C$. If a vector bundle $E$ of rank $e$ satisfies $\\mu_{\\max}(E|_C)\\le M$ with $M\\ge0$, then $$\\label{eq:diag-flag-bound}\n h^0(W,E)\\le e(M+1)\\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}(1+M/d_i).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-diag-flag",

If a vector bundle $E$ of rank $e$ satisfies $\\mu_{\\max}(E|_C)\\le M$ with $M\\ge0$, then $$\\label{eq:diag-flag-bound}\n h^0(W,E)\\le e(M+1)\\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}(1+M/d_i).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diag-orders",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W_i$ be integral projective varieties, $x_i$ smooth points, and $Q_i$ line bundles with nonzero section spaces, for $1\\le i\\le t$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Iitaka function field** ([source](#sec:introduction)): To specify the required conclusion, let $K(K_X)\subset k(X)$ be the field generated by ratios of nonzero sections in the same positive pluricanonical degree, over all such degrees.
- **Defines the Iitaka fibration** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A complete system *defines the Iitaka fibration* if it is nonempty and its section ratios generate $K(K_X)$.
- **Rational Cartier divisor** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A *rational Cartier divisor* means a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier $\mathbb Q$-divisor.
- **Divisor comparison** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For rational divisors $D,E$ we write $D\preceq E$ if $E-D$ is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective divisor.
- **Common trivializing multiple** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): An index bound is a *common trivializing multiple*: a single integer kills all the actual divisor classes under consideration and clears their coefficients.
- **Contraction** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): A *contraction* is a projective surjective morphism $f:X\to Z$ of normal varieties such that $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$.
- **Quasi-étale map** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A finite map of normal varieties is *quasi-étale* if it is étale outside a set of codimension at least two.
- **Canonical index** ([source](#sec:reductions)): For a normal projective variety with $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, its *canonical index* is the least positive integer $r$ such that $rK_X$ is an integral principal divisor.
- **Section-order invariant** ([source](#sec:scalar)): For a big, nef, semiample rational Cartier divisor $P$ on an integral projective variety $V$ and a smooth point $x\in V$, define $$\gamma(P;V,x)=\sup_{\substack{k>0\text{ sufficiently divisible}\\ 0\ne s\in H^0(\widetilde V,k\mu^*P)}} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x(s)}{k},$$ where $\mu:\widetilde V\to V$ is a smooth projective resolution that is an isomorphism near $x$, and the same letter denotes its lift.
- **Seshadri constant** ([source](#sec:scalar)): For an ample $P$, also put $$\varepsilon(P;x)=\inf_{\substack{x\in C\subset V\\C\text{ integral curve}}} \frac{P\cdot C}{\operatorname{mult}_x C}.$$
- **Relative form weight** ([source](#pm-h-13)): More generally, for a relative pluricanonical form $\omega$ of degree $m$ define $$\label{eq:den-weight} w_z(\omega)=\inf_E \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(\omega\wedge dz/z)+m}{m\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(z)}.$$
- **Truncated symmetric algebra** ([source](#pm-h-27)): In characteristic $p>0$, let $A_\bullet(E)$ be the symmetric algebra modulo the ideal generated locally by $p$-th powers of degree-one elements.

```json
            "exact_quote": "Let $k$ be a field of characteristic zero and let $P=\\prod_{i=1}^t B_i$ be a product of geometrically integral projective factors whose geometric fibres are positive dimensional blocks as above; include the abelian part, when present, as a single factor. Let $R\\to P$ be a geometrically integral normal finite quasi-étale cover, and put $d=\\dim R$. If a finite group $G$ acts on $R$, then there is a subgroup $G_0$ of index at most $d!$ and connected finite quasi-étale covers $R_i\\to B_i$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$R\\to\\prod_iR_i$ is finite and quasi-étale;*\n\n2.  *$G_0$ acts regularly on each $R_i$, and the projections $R\\to R_i$ are equivariant;*\n\n3.  *a generating volume form on $R$ is proportional to the wedge product of the pulled-back volume generators on the $R_i$.*\n\n*The conclusions also hold for finite semilinear actions, interpreting a factor transformation as an isomorphism to the corresponding field-conjugate variety. In particular the $d!$th power of any finite-order transformation preserves all factor fields.",
            "source_id": "PM-034.10"
          }
        ],

          }
```

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-denominator) / [proof](#sec:denominators): Control block characters after semistable degeneration. -> Uniform moduli denominator
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-index-reduction) / [proof](#sec:reductions): Separate product covers and reduce to terminal counterexamples. -> Terminal high-index sequence
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar) / [proof](#sec:scalar): Track small-volume members and their Iitaka fibres. -> Scalar section-order and Seshadri bounds
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-diagonal) / [proof](#sec:diagonal): Compare Frobenius ranks and determinant orders. -> Quadratic diagonal-product bound
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-index-contradiction) / [proof](#sec:transport): Construct compatible leaves and rational recovery of coordinates. -> Zero-boundary klt index bound
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-lc-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lc-reduction): Use normalized boundary adjunction and a field norm. -> Normal lc index bound
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-all-degree-2): Compare all pluricanonical degrees and use base effective birationality. -> Full Iitaka field in a uniform degree
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-31): Descend finite defining data and compare section fields after algebraically closed extensions. -> The same dimension-dependent degree over every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

The groups $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bir}}(V_\\nu)$ are countable, and there are ample rational Cartier divisors $L_\\nu$ and constants $c,C_2>0$, independent of $\\nu$, such that $$\\varepsilon(L_\\nu)\\ge c,\\qquad\n \\varepsilon(\\pi_\\nu^*L_\\nu)\\ge cr_\\nu^{1/n}.$$ For $t\\ge2$, let $Z_{\\nu,t}=Y_\\nu^t/G_{\\nu,\\mathrm{diag}}$, let $\\theta_{\\nu,t}:Z_{\\nu,t}\\to V_\\nu^t$ be the finite quotient map, and set $P_{\\nu,t}=\\theta_{\\nu,t}^*L_\\nu^{\\boxplus t}$. The final condition is $$\\varepsilon(P_{\\nu,t})\\le C_2t^2$$ for every fixed $t$, on a tail allowed to depend on $t$. All Seshadri constants are at very general points.",

Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties, where $\\dim X=n$, $\\dim Z>0$, $X$ is klt, and $K_X$ is rationally linearly equivalent to the pullback of a rational Cartier divisor on $Z$. Then there are a rational function $\\psi\\in\\mathbb C(X)^*$ and canonical bundle formula data satisfying the equality of rational divisors $$\\label{eq:den-exact}\n K_X+\\frac{1}{p_0}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}(\\psi)=f^*D_Z,\n \\qquad D_Z=K_Z+B_Z+M_Z.$$ Here $B_Z\\geq0$ has coefficients in a rational DCC set depending only on $n$, the generalized pair $(Z,B_Z+M_Z)$ is generalized klt, and the moduli b-divisor $\\mathbf M$ is b-nef with $p\\mathbf M$ b-Cartier.",

```json
          "exact_quote": "If the canonical indices of projective klt $n$-folds with $K_X\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$ are unbounded, there is a sequence of projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal $n$-folds $V_i$, of canonical indices $r_i\\to\\infty$, satisfying the following properties.*\n\n1.  *A smooth projective resolution of $V_i$ has irregularity zero, and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bir}}(V_i)$ is countable.*\n\n2.  *Every positive-dimensional rational image of $V_i$ of dimension less than $n$ has rationally connected smooth projective resolution.*\n\n3.  *Every effective Weil divisor on $V_i$ with positive Iitaka dimension is big.*\n\n4.  *Let $\\pi_i:Y_i\\to V_i$ be the cyclic index cover, with its deck group $G_i$. Then $Y_i$ is terminal and $K_{Y_i}\\sim0$. Every $G_i$-equivariant rational fibration of $Y_i$ having positive-dimensional base and fibre has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. The same assertion holds for rational fibrations of $V_i$, with trivial group action.*\n\n*Here a rational fibration has geometrically integral generic fibre, or equivalently has a relatively algebraically closed base function field.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R2",
      "inputs": [
        "Uniform moduli denominator",
        "Rationally connected base torsion"
      ],
      "method": "Separate product covers and reduce to terminal counterexamples.",
      "output": "Terminal high-index sequence",
      "proof_anchor": "sec:reductions"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar",
          "exact_quote": "Assume $I_j$ for $j<n$. There are constants $C_n,c_n>0$ with the following property. Let $V$ be a normal projective canonical $n$-fold with $K_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$, and let a finite group $G$ act on $V$. Suppose that every $G$-equivariant rational fibration with geometrically integral generic fibre and positive-dimensional base and fibre has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. If $P$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor with $g^*P\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}P$ for every $g\\in G$, then $$\\label{eq:scalar-estimates}\n       \\gamma(P;V)\\le C_n(P^n)^{1/n},\n       \\qquad \\varepsilon(P)\\ge c_n(P^n)^{1/n}.$$ The constants are independent of $V$, $G$, and the denominator of $P$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R3",
      "inputs": [
        "Lower-dimensional effective Iitaka fibrations",
        "Generic chains and tracking"
      ],
      "method": "Track small-volume members and their Iitaka fibres.",
      "output": "Scalar section-order and Seshadri bounds",
      "proof_anchor": "sec:scalar"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-diagonal",
      "depends_on": [
        "R3"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-diagonal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a normal projective canonical complex variety of dimension $n\\ge2$, with $K_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$ and canonical index $r$. Let $\\pi:Y\\to V$ be its canonical cyclic cover. Suppose that $L$ is an ample rational Cartier divisor and $C_1>0$ is rational, with $$1\\le L^n\\le2,\\qquad \\gamma(L;V)\\le C_1.$$ Choose a positive rational number $b_0$ such that $$\\label{eq:diag-b0}\n 3(2b_0)^n<\\frac14,\\qquad b_0(C_1+1)<\\frac34.$$ For every integer $t\\ge2$, set $$Z_t=Y^t/\\mu_{r,\\mathrm{diag}},\\qquad\n \\theta_t:Z_t\\longrightarrow V^t,\\qquad\n P_t=\\theta_t^*L^{\\boxplus t}.$$ Then $P_t$ is ample and $$\\label{eq:diag-epsilon}\n \\varepsilon(P_t)\\le\\frac{(n+1)t^2}{b_0}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R4",
      "inputs": [
        "Scalar section-order bound",
        "Truncated-power slopes",
        "Fixed flags"
      ],
      "method": "Compare Frobenius ranks and determinant orders.",
      "output": "Quadratic diagonal-product bound",
      "proof_anchor": "sec:diagonal"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-index-contradiction",
      "depends_on": [
        "R2",
        "R3",
        "R4"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-index-contradiction",
          "exact_quote": "There is no sequence of finite surjective covers $\\pi_\\nu:Y_\\nu\\to V_\\nu$ of normal projective complex $n$-folds, generically torsors for cyclic groups $G_\\nu$ of orders $r_\\nu\\to\\infty$, satisfying the following conditions. The groups $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bir}}(V_\\nu)$ are countable, and there are ample rational Cartier divisors $L_\\nu$ and constants $c,C_2>0$, independent of $\\nu$, such that $$\\varepsilon(L_\\nu)\\ge c,\\qquad\n \\varepsilon(\\pi_\\nu^*L_\\nu)\\ge cr_\\nu^{1/n}.$$ For $t\\ge2$, let $Z_{\\nu,t}=Y_\\nu^t/G_{\\nu,\\mathrm{diag}}$, let $\\theta_{\\nu,t}:Z_{\\nu,t}\\to V_\\nu^t$ be the finite quotient map, and set $P_{\\nu,t}=\\theta_{\\nu,t}^*L_\\nu^{\\boxplus t}$. The final condition is $$\\varepsilon(P_{\\nu,t})\\le C_2t^2$$ for every fixed $t$, on a tail allowed to depend on $t$. All Seshadri constants are at very general points.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.10"
        }
```

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **INV-IITAKA**: A pluricanonical degree depending only on dimension defines the Iitaka fibration throughout the stated geometric regime.
- **INV-FIELD**: Defining the Iitaka fibration requires nonemptiness and generation of the full Iitaka function field by section ratios.
- **INV-INDEX**: A common integer multiple of the log canonical divisor is an integral principal divisor.
- **INV-DENOMINATOR**: The moduli b-divisor admits a uniform b-Cartier multiple depending only on the total-space dimension.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **ANTI-NUMERIC-DEGREE**: Select a practical pluricanonical degree from the uniform existence theorem. -> Existence does not supply a numerical degree for implementation.
- **ANTI-IMAGE**: Accept the expected image dimension as certification of the Iitaka fibration. -> The section ratios may generate a proper finite subfield.
- **ANTI-CHARACTERISTIC**: Apply the uniform degree theorem in positive characteristic. -> The stated field hypothesis is unavailable.
- **ANTI-NUMERICAL-TORSION**: Replace the rational linear triviality hypothesis by numerical triviality. -> The required principalization hypothesis is lost.
- **ANTI-HORIZONTAL**: Extend the denominator argument to a fibration with horizontal boundary. -> The factor-form and degeneration arguments no longer have their stated hypotheses.
- **ANTI-NONNORMAL**: Treat the normal lc reduction as trivialization on the full reducible boundary scheme. -> A field norm from a normalized component does not establish that scheme-level trivialization.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The uniform pluricanonical degree is explicitly existential: no small or practical numerical value of $m(d)$ is asserted. The result requires geometric section spaces and equality of their full Iitaka function field; a finite arithmetic check of an auxiliary bound does not construct that degree or certify this field equality. A finite executable construction for these conclusions is ABSENT.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The uniform pluricanonical degree requires a smooth integral projective variety of nonnegative Kodaira dimension over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to positive characteristic, negative Kodaira dimension, or varieties outside its geometric hypotheses.']
- **L2** (nonconstructive): The uniform degree is existential; the paper does not assert a practical numerical value. Blocked use: ['Selecting an explicit computational degree from the existence theorem alone.', 'Claiming a practical runtime bound for computing the Iitaka fibration.']
- **L3** (formal_scope): Defining the Iitaka fibration requires a nonempty complete system whose section ratios generate the full Iitaka function field. Blocked use: ['Replacing full function-field generation with the dimension of the rational image.', 'Accepting a proper finite subfield as the complete Iitaka field.']
- **L4** (edge_case): In Kodaira dimension zero the asserted conclusion is nonemptiness with point image. Blocked use: ['Interpreting this case as a positive-dimensional fibration or a birational embedding.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): The log canonical index theorem fixes the dimension and a rational boundary coefficient set satisfying the descending chain condition. Blocked use: ['Dropping control of the coefficient set.', 'Extending the conclusion to arbitrary real boundaries.']
- **L6** (formal_scope): The index conclusion is an integral principal multiple of the actual divisor. Blocked use: ['Substituting numerical triviality for the principal-divisor conclusion.', 'Treating a Cartier denominator bound as a torsion trivialization.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The proof uses the companion good-log-minimal-model theorem with nonvanishing in all dimensions. Blocked use: ['Presenting the proof as independent of the stated log-abundance input.', 'Deriving this input from finite generation for big adjoints alone.']
- **L8** (formal_scope): The normal lc reduction uses a normalized boundary component and a field norm; it does not trivialize the log canonical sheaf on the full reducible boundary. Blocked use: ['Extending the reduction to a nonnormal index theorem without further justification.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The relative denominator argument requires absence of horizontal boundary. Blocked use: ['Applying the denominator statement to arbitrary horizontal boundary data.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): The degeneration argument bounds the volume-form characters entering the moduli divisor, without a bound on semistable ramification. Blocked use: ['Claiming a uniform bound on the ramification degree of semistable reduction.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): The relative torsion corollary assumes rational linear triviality of the total canonical divisor and rational connectedness of a smooth resolution of the base. Blocked use: ['Replacing rational linear triviality of the total canonical divisor with numerical triviality.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The scalar estimates require general-type smooth geometric generic fibres for every intermediate equivariant rational fibration. Blocked use: ['Using the scalar bounds for arbitrary canonical varieties with rationally trivial canonical divisor while omitting the fibration condition.']
- **L13** (formal_scope): The chain estimates concern complete section spaces on resolutions of members. Blocked use: ['Checking only restrictions of ambient sections when applying the chain bounds to Iitaka fibres.']
- **L14** (formal_scope): The transport construction does not assert commutativity of its rational vector fields. Blocked use: ['Treating the ordered local flows as a commuting action without additional proof.']
- **L15** (unknown): An executable engineering correspondence beyond the stated geometric setting is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Transferring the theorem to a software or physical model without establishing its geometric hypotheses.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the effective Iitaka fibration conjecture in characteristic zero. For each dimension, one pluricanonical degree defines the Iitaka fibration of every smooth integral projective variety of that dimension and nonnegative Kodaira dimension over an algebraically closed field. The associated sections generate the full Iitaka function field.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a smooth integral projective variety $X$, the pluricanonical systems organize the birational information carried by its canonical divisor. If some $H^0(X,\mathcal O_X(mK_X))$ is nonzero, the maximum of the dimensions of the resulting rational images is the Kodaira dimension $\kappa(X)$. In sufficiently large divisible degrees these maps determine the Iitaka fibration <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Iitaka1971](#ref-Iitaka1971)]. The question here is whether divisibility and largeness can be prescribed using only $\dim X$.

To specify the required conclusion, let $K(K_X)\subset k(X)$ be the field generated by ratios of nonzero sections in the same positive pluricanonical degree, over all such degrees. This is the function field of the Iitaka base. A complete system *defines the Iitaka fibration* if it is nonempty and its section ratios generate $K(K_X)$. Requiring only an image of dimension $\kappa(X)$ would be weaker: it would allow a proper finite subfield of the Iitaka field.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Uniform pluricanonical degree). *For each integer $d\geq1$ there is an integer $m(d)>0$ such that, over every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, the complete system $|m(d)K_X|$ defines the Iitaka fibration of every smooth integral projective $d$-fold $X$ with $\kappa(X)\geq0$.*

</div>

Using the log-abundance input from <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA)], this gives an affirmative resolution of the effective Iitaka fibration conjecture <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BirkarZhang2016](#ref-BirkarZhang2016), Conjecture 1.1]. Every positive multiple of $m(d)$ works as well, by the section-ratio argument in Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">9</a>. Thus the same degree works for all nonnegative Kodaira dimensions. In the general-type case its map is birational. In Kodaira dimension zero the conclusion is nonemptiness in that degree, with image a point. The theorem is existential; no small or practical numerical value of $m(d)$ is asserted.

The proof establishes a second uniform statement at the same time. A pair in the next theorem is normal and integral; its boundary is effective and its log canonical divisor is rational Cartier.

<a id="stmt-thm-lc-index"></a>
<div id="thm:lc-index" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Uniform log canonical index). *Let $d\geq0$, and let $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$ satisfy the descending chain condition. There is an integer $a(d,\Phi)>0$ such that every projective log canonical pair $(X,B)$ of dimension $d$, with boundary coefficients in $\Phi$ and $K_X+B\sim_\mathbb Q0$, satisfies $$a(d,\Phi)(K_X+B)\sim0.$$ Here the multiple is an integral principal divisor, not merely a numerically trivial or rationally linearly trivial divisor.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and preceding work

Effective pluricanonical maps are a quantitative part of birational classification. The general-type case asks for a uniform birational degree and was established by Hacon–McKernan, Takayama, and Tsuji <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HaconMcKernan2006](#ref-HaconMcKernan2006), [Takayama2006](#ref-Takayama2006), [Tsuji2007](#ref-Tsuji2007)]. Hacon–McKernan–Xu’s theorem for big log canonical adjoints permits boundary coefficients in a fixed DCC set <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[HMX2014](#ref-HMX2014), Theorem 1.3]. The lower-Kodaira-dimensional problem has an additional feature: the general fibre contributes torsion and monodromy data which do not appear in the big case.

Fujino–Mori obtained a uniform pluricanonical degree for threefolds of Kodaira dimension one using their canonical bundle formula <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[FujinoMori2000](#ref-FujinoMori2000), Corollary 6.2]. Their denominator argument identifies finite characters on an extended Hodge line and bounds their orders using the middle Betti number of a canonical cover <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[FujinoMori2000](#ref-FujinoMori2000), Theorem 3.1 and Sections 3.6–3.8]. Viehweg–Zhang obtained effective results for Iitaka bases of dimension two, with constants controlled by invariants of the general fibre <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ViehwegZhang2009](#ref-ViehwegZhang2009), Theorem 0.2]. Birkar–Zhang proved a general effective Iitaka theorem with dependence on the dimension, the least nonvanishing canonical degree of the general fibre, and a middle Betti number of a smooth model of its canonical cover <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BirkarZhang2016](#ref-BirkarZhang2016), Theorem 1.2]. Their effective birationality theorem for polarized adjoints is the final effective input used here <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BirkarZhang2016](#ref-BirkarZhang2016), Theorem 1.3]. The present argument supplies the required denominator control without assuming a uniform bound for those middle Betti numbers.

The index problem has its own inductive structure. Xu relates zero-boundary klt index bounds to bounds with boundary <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Xu2019](#ref-Xu2019), Theorem 1.8]. Birkar proves the index theorem for rationally connected klt Calabi–Yau pairs in every fixed dimension <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Birkar2025](#ref-Birkar2025), Corollary 1.8]; his boundedness theorem for generalized Calabi–Yau pairs also controls torsion on rationally connected bases <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Birkar2025](#ref-Birkar2025), Theorem 1.7]. We combine these results with an arithmetic Stein-degree theorem <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[SD](#ref-SD), Main Theorem] to pass from klt pairs to normal lc pairs without an index theorem for nonnormal pairs.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Inputs and contributions

One major input must be distinguished from standard minimal-model technology. We use the good-log-minimal-model theorem of the companion *Log abundance in characteristic zero <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA), Theorem 11.1]* , stated precisely in Theorem <a href="#thm:good-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:good-models">3</a>. It includes nonvanishing and is used in all dimensions. It is not being inferred from finite generation for big adjoints. The other companion inputs are the chain and tracking lemmas in <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[U4](#ref-U4)], and the arithmetic Stein-degree bound in <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[SD](#ref-SD)]. The arguments of <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[R4](#ref-R4)] provide the preceding fourfold treatment of relative denominators and rationally connected torsion.

The proof extends the canonical-index method of <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[U4](#ref-U4)] to arbitrary dimension. There are two substantial extensions. First, we prove a relative denominator bound by decomposing the geometric generic fibre, degenerating its individual factors, and controlling their volume-form characters on normal components of a dlt special fibre. This separates the potentially unbounded ramification of semistable reduction from the bounded characters that enter the moduli divisor. The argument uses the absence of horizontal boundary and only lower-dimensional normal lc index bounds. It is therefore a reusable relative statement, not merely the final step of the Iitaka theorem.

Second, the small-volume argument in <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[U4](#ref-U4)] is extended to Iitaka bases of arbitrary dimension. Weak positivity supplies a fibre-to-total volume comparison, while flattening ensures that codimension-two losses on the base create no divisorial poles on the original smooth model. This gives scalar bounds for section orders and Seshadri constants. We then give the dimension-dependent calculations in the Frobenius diagonal argument and in the compatible-chain argument. Those constructions are adaptations of <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[U4](#ref-U4)]; the new claim is their use in the all-dimensional induction, not a new origin for the constructions themselves.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The induction

We prove Theorems <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> and <a href="#thm:lc-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:lc-index">2</a> simultaneously. Fix $n$ and suppose both are known in smaller dimensions. The relative step starts with a contraction $f:X\to Z$ from a klt $n$-fold, with $\dim Z>0$ and $K_X$ rationally linearly pulled back from $Z$. The canonical bundle formula writes $$K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}f^*(K_Z+B_Z+M_Z),$$ where $B_Z$ is the discriminant divisor and $M_Z$ is the moduli divisor. Section <a href="#sec:denominators" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:denominators">4</a> bounds a Cartier multiple of the moduli divisor on a birational base model using only lower-dimensional normal lc indices. When $K_X\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$ and the base has a rationally connected smooth resolution, this denominator bound combines with torsion control on the base to bound the canonical index of $X$.

Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">5</a> then reduces failure of the zero-boundary klt index bound to terminal varieties $V$ with canonical indices $r\to\infty$ and countable birational groups. Their canonical cyclic covers $\pi:Y\to V$ have degree $r$ and deck group $G$. Every $G$-equivariant rational fibration of $Y$ with positive-dimensional base and fibre has general-type smooth geometric generic fibre. We call such fibrations intermediate. The corresponding assertion holds for intermediate rational fibrations of $V$.

Choose an ample rational Cartier divisor $L$ on $V$ with $1\le L^n\le2$. For an ample rational Cartier divisor $P$, write $\varepsilon(P)$ for its very general Seshadri constant: the infimum of $P\cdot C/\operatorname{mult}_xC$ over integral curves through a very general point $x$. Section <a href="#sec:scalar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scalar">6</a> uses lower-dimensional effective Iitaka fibrations to bound section orders on $V$ and bound $\varepsilon(L)$ below uniformly. The pullback polarization has $(\pi^*L)^n=rL^n$.

For $t\ge2$, let $Z_t=Y^t/G_{\mathrm{diag}}$, where $G$ acts by the same deck transformation in every slot. Let $\theta_t:Z_t\to V^t$ be the induced finite map and set $P_t=\theta_t^*(\sum_{i=1}^t\operatorname{pr}_i^*L)$. Together, the scalar estimates and the diagonal argument of Section <a href="#sec:diagonal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagonal">7</a> give $$\varepsilon(\pi^*L)\ge c r^{1/n},
       \qquad \varepsilon(P_t)\le Ct^2,$$ with $c,C>0$ depending only on $n$. The second bound supplies curves of bounded $P_t$-degree divided by marked multiplicity.

Section <a href="#sec:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transport">8</a> arranges these curves into chains compatible with coordinate projections. The resulting positive-dimensional leaves project generically finitely onto their images in each coordinate, and comparison of their projection degrees gives a degree-one coordinate-forgetting map. Rational recovery of its missing coordinate turns local flows into uncountably many birational self-maps of $V$, a contradiction. This bounds the zero-boundary klt index. The normal lc reduction in Section <a href="#sec:index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:index">3</a> then proves Theorem <a href="#thm:lc-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:lc-index">2</a> in dimension $n$. Finally, Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">9</a> reuses the denominator bound with polarized effective birationality to prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> in that dimension. Figure <a href="#fig:induction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:induction">1</a> records the dependencies after the induction statements have been formally introduced.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Conventions and the induction hypotheses

Until the final field-descent argument we work over $\mathbb C$. Varieties are integral and projective unless another setting is stated. We use the standard discrepancy conventions for klt, dlt, and lc pairs <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Kollar2013](#ref-Kollar2013)]. All boundaries in the proof are rational. A *rational Cartier divisor* means a $\mathbb Q$-Cartier $\mathbb Q$-divisor. Canonical divisors are chosen compatibly when forms are pulled back or residues are taken. On a normal variety, sections of an integral Weil divisor always mean sections of its rank-one reflexive divisorial sheaf.

For rational divisors $D,E$ we write $D\preceq E$ if $E-D$ is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective divisor. An index bound is a *common trivializing multiple*: a single integer kills all the actual divisor classes under consideration and clears their coefficients. A bound on the least possible integers implies a common multiple by taking their least common multiple.

A *contraction* is a projective surjective morphism $f:X\to Z$ of normal varieties such that $f_*\mathcal O_X=\mathcal O_Z$. Its generic fibre is geometrically integral in characteristic zero. A rational fibration is understood through a relatively algebraically closed subfield of the total function field; after resolution and Stein factorization it has connected fibres. A smooth geometric generic model is a smooth projective model of the geometric generic fibre.

For generalized pairs, the nef data consist of a nef rational Cartier divisor on a higher birational model, together with its compatible pullbacks. A moduli b-divisor is *b-nef and b-Cartier* if it is the Cartier closure of a nef divisor on one such model. The generalized discrepancies are computed by pulling back the full adjoint, including these nef data. We use the conventions of <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[BirkarZhang2016](#ref-BirkarZhang2016)].

<a id="stmt-thm-good-models"></a>
<div id="thm:good-models" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Good minimal models; <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA)]). *Let $(X,B)$ be a projective lc pair over $\mathbb C$ with effective rational boundary and pseudo-effective $K_X+B$. Then $(X,B)$ has a good log minimal model. In particular, its adjoint is nonvanishing, and on the good model the adjoint is semiample.*

</div>

This is the rational-boundary case of <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA), Theorem 11.1]. We also use the standard klt MMP and its discrepancy comparisons <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[BCHM2010](#ref-BCHM2010)]. For klt pairs with a good minimal model, an MMP with scaling can be chosen terminating at such a model. We will specify relative or equivariant uses when they arise; they do not require a separate unmentioned abundance assumption.

<a id="stmt-not-induction"></a>
<div id="not:induction" class="notation">

**Notation 4** (Induction statements). Let $I_n$ denote Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> in dimension $n$, and let $L_n$ denote Theorem <a href="#thm:lc-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:lc-index">2</a> in dimension $n$, for every rational DCC coefficient set. Let $K_n$ denote its zero-boundary klt case: there is a common integer $a_n>0$ with $a_nK_X\sim0$ for all projective klt $n$-folds satisfying $K_X\sim_\mathbb Q0$.

</div>

The dimension-zero statements hold with degree one. In a fixed induction step we assume $I_j,L_j$ for all $j<n$. By global ACC <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[HMX2014](#ref-HMX2014), Theorem 1.5], the coefficients of numerically trivial lc pairs of fixed dimension with coefficients in a fixed DCC set belong to a finite subset. Thus proving $L_n$ for every finite rational coefficient set proves its stated DCC form. This observation also applies to the different coefficients that occur in adjunction.

The dependency diagram below distinguishes $K_n$ from $L_n$. In particular, the proof of the moduli denominator uses only $L_j$ with $j<n$, not the dimension-$n$ index statement being established.

<figure id="fig:induction">

<figcaption>One induction step. The left-hand route supplies the relative index control used in reducing $K_{n}$; the right-hand route supplies its section-order estimates. After $K_{n}$ is proved, adjunction and Stein degree give $L_{n}$, and the denominator bound also feeds the final proof of $I_{n}$, as shown by the direct left-hand arrow. Arrows record the main dependencies, not morphisms of varieties.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-induction"></a>

```latex
 arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=2mm]},semithick}]
\node[box] (lowL) at (0,0) {$L_j$, $j<n$\\normal lc indices};
\node[box] (lowI) at (6,0) {$I_j$, $j<n$\\effective Iitaka maps};
\node[box,text width=43mm] (den) at (0,-2) {Moduli denominators\\torsion over rationally\\connected bases};
\node[box] (scalar) at (6,-2) {Small volumes\\and scalar estimates};
\node[box,text width=57mm] (idx) at (6,-4) {$K_n$: zero-boundary klt index\\diagonal products and chains};
\node[box] (lc) at (6,-6) {$L_n$\\adjunction and Stein degree};
\node[box] (iit) at (0,-6) {$I_n$\\denominators and base birationality};
\draw[arr] (lowL)--(den);
\draw[arr] (lowI)--(scalar);
\draw[arr] (den)--(idx);
\draw[arr] (scalar)--(idx);
\draw[arr] (idx)--(lc);
\draw[arr] (lc)--(iit);
\draw[arr] (den)--(iit);
\end{tikzpicture}
```

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Geometric generic fibres and fields of definition

All finite collections of varieties, maps, divisors, and rational forms can be defined over a finitely generated field of characteristic zero. Algebraically closed extensions preserve the singularity conditions, dimensions of section spaces, Cartierness, and triviality of the line bundles used here. For singularities one may spread a log resolution and its discrepancy calculation; for sections and trivializations one uses flat base change and faithful flatness. Consequently the induction hypotheses apply to geometric generic fibres, by comparison with an algebraically closed field embedded in $\mathbb C$.

We occasionally need very general points to satisfy countably many section-order conditions simultaneously. One may make these choices over $\mathbb C$, or use points geometric generic over a countable field of definition for the varieties and all the systems involved. Moving members below are taken at geometric generic parameters. Dominant extensions needed to define models or maps do not specialize a previously chosen generic parameter.

We record one descent observation used repeatedly. If $F$ is a geometrically integral normal projective variety over a field $K$ and a given reflexive pluricanonical sheaf becomes the trivial line bundle over $\overline K$, then it is already a line bundle over $K$ and is trivial there. Local freeness descends faithfully flatly. Base change identifies its space of sections with a one-dimensional space after extension, and any nonzero section over $K$ becomes a generator, hence is a generator before extension as well. The same argument applies to a specified integral log pluricanonical divisor.

<a id="sec:index"></a>
### Normal log canonical indices and finite covers

Two preliminary arguments will keep the induction within normal varieties. The first recovers an lc index from the index of one normalized boundary component and its Stein degree. The second separates finite actions on the factors of a quasi-étale product cover. Throughout this section, the inductive assertions $I_j,L_j$ are available for $j<n$; the assertion $K_n$ is used only in Proposition <a href="#prop:lc-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:lc-reduction">6</a>.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Descent from a normalized boundary component
